People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

office and I remember the police being there, but the middle part—what he said right before I reached for the phone—is just a blank no matter how hard I try to pull it up.\n- key: S076\n  name: Inability_Positive_Emotions\n  description: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).\n  manifestation: My wife tries to joke with me or asks about going fishing like we planned, and I know those things should feel good, but it's like the part of me that enjoys them has been switched off.\n- key: S079\n  name: the porch, and where the exits are, like I need to be ready for someone to come at me again.\n- key: S080\n  name: Exaggerated_Startle_Response\n  description: Exaggerated startle response to minor myself to handle returns or keep the workplace safe right now.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 weeks\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two weeks, ever since that client threatened me in my office.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 54 / tax_preparer / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S082\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say depressed exactly… I mean, I’m getting through the day. But things don’t feel like they should. My wife will try to joke with me, or bring up going fishing like we’d planned, and I know I should feel happy about that, or at least look forward to it. It’s just like that part of me is sort of switched off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, but after that happened I started having these moments where even my own house or my office didn’t feel quite real… like I was sort of watching myself from across the room instead of just being there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people or social situations since the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out of my way to cut people off or anything like that. Maybe I’ve been a little quieter than usual, but I’m still seeing people when I need to. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things to avoid reminders of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been doing that on purpose, no. I mean, if something comes up that reminds me of him, I don’t love it, but I haven’t really changed my routine just to avoid it. At least I don’t think so. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "content": "Return the JSON now."

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.1, F45.3, F48.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if I’m explaining it right, but… I’ve been having these strange moments where my own house, or sometimes my office, just doesn’t feel real. Like I’m there, but also kind of watching myself from across the room. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but it’s unsettling enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say depressed exactly… I mean, I’m getting through the day. But things don’t feel like they should. My wife will try to joke with me, or bring up going fishing like we’d planned, and I know I should feel happy about that, or at least look forward to it. It’s just like that part of me is sort of switched off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m definitely more… keyed up than usual. Even at home in Roanoke, I catch myself listening for every car door, footsteps on the porch, that kind of thing. I’ll notice where the exits are too, like I need to be ready in case someone comes at me again. I know that probably sounds odd, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… not like hearing voices or seeing people who aren’t there. Nothing like that, as far as I can tell. I’ve just felt off, but I haven’t noticed anything that wasn’t actually there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these strange moments where things don't feel real?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two weeks now… maybe a little under or right around that. It started after that client threatened me in my office. I don’t want to make too much of it, but since then I’ve had those moments where things just feel kind of unreal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places or activities since the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m avoiding things, exactly. I mean, I still go where I need to go. I guess I’ve been a little more selective, maybe taking an extra moment before going certain places, but… I don’t know if that counts. I haven’t really noticed any big changes in what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Did the feeling of things not being real start immediately after the client threatened you, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: It was pretty soon after, I think. I don’t want to overstate it, but after that happened I started having these moments where even my own house or my office didn’t feel quite real… like I was sort of watching myself from across the room instead of just being there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people or social situations since the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out of my way to cut people off or anything like that. Maybe I’ve been a little quieter than usual, but I’m still seeing people when I need to. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things to avoid reminders of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been doing that on purpose, no. I mean, if something comes up that reminds me of him, I don’t love it, but I haven’t really changed my routine just to avoid it. At least I don’t think so. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been making an effort to avoid thinking about the incident with the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m making an effort exactly. I mean, I don’t sit there trying to think about it, of course, but I haven’t noticed myself deliberately pushing it away or anything. If it comes up, I just kind of… move on, I guess. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking or thinking about the incident with the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’m not eager to bring it up, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding it in some big way. If someone asks, like you are, I can talk about it. I just don’t want to make too much of it, I guess. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself going out of your way to avoid things that remind you of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not thrilled when something reminds me of him, but I haven’t been, like, changing where I go or what I do because of it. At least not that I’ve noticed. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything to avoid memories or thoughts of the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any deliberate way. I mean, I’m not trying to sit around replaying it, but I haven’t noticed myself doing anything special to block it out or avoid the memories. If it pops up, I just try to get back to whatever I was doing. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been significantly changing your daily routine to avoid reminders of the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve been changing my routine in any significant way. I still go about my day pretty much the same. I mean, I might feel a little uneasy if something brings it to mind, but I’m not rearranging my life around it or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but it’s unsettling enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say depressed exactly… I mean, I’m getting through the day. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m definitely more… keyed up than usual. Even at home in Roanoke, I catch myself listening for every car door, footsteps on the porch, that kind of thing. I’ll notice where the exits are too, like I need to be ready in case someone comes at me again. It’s probably not as bad as it sounds, but it’s unsettling enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say depressed exactly… I mean, I’m getting through the day. But things don’t feel like they should. My wife will try to joke with me, or bring up going fishing like we’d planned, and I know I should feel happy about that, or at least look forward to it. It’s just like that part of me is sort of switched off. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’m definitely more… keyed up than usual. Even at home in Roanoke, I catch myself listening for every car door, footsteps on the porch, that kind of thing. I’ll notice where the exits are too, like I need to be ready in case someone comes at me again. I know that probably sounds odd, but that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… not like hearing voices or seeing people who aren’t there. Nothing like that, as far as I can tell. I’ve just felt off, but I haven’t noticed anything that wasn’t actually there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these strange moments where things don't feel real?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been about two weeks now… maybe a little under or right around that. It started after that client threatened me in my office. I don’t want to make too much of it, but since then I’ve had those moments where things just feel kind of unreal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places or activities since the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m avoiding things, exactly. I mean, I still go where I need to go. I guess I’ve been a little more selective, maybe taking an extra moment before going certain places, but… I don’t know if that counts. I haven’t really noticed any big changes in what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Did the feeling of things not being real start immediately after the client threatened you, or was there a delay?\n\nPatient: It was pretty soon after, I think. I don’t want to overstate it, but after that happened I started having these moments where even my own house or my office didn’t feel quite real… like I was sort of watching myself from across the room instead of just being there. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people or social situations since the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I haven’t been going out of my way to cut people off or anything like that. Maybe I’ve been a little quieter than usual, but I’m still seeing people when I need to. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing things to avoid reminders of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve been doing that on purpose, no. I mean, if something comes up that reminds me of him, I don’t love it, but I haven’t really changed my routine just to avoid it. At least I don’t think so. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been making an effort to avoid thinking about the incident with the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m making an effort exactly. I mean, I don’t sit there trying to think about it, of course, but I haven’t noticed myself deliberately pushing it away or anything. If it comes up, I just kind of… move on, I guess. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding talking or thinking about the incident with the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’m not eager to bring it up, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding it in some big way. If someone asks, like you are, I can talk about it. I just don’t want to make too much of it, I guess. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself going out of your way to avoid things that remind you of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not thrilled when something reminds me of him, but I haven’t been, like, changing where I go or what I do because of it. At least not that I’ve noticed. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything to avoid memories or thoughts of the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any deliberate way. I mean, I’m not trying to sit around replaying it, but I haven’t noticed myself doing anything special to block it out or avoid the memories. If it pops up, I just try to get back to whatever I was doing. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been significantly changing your daily routine to avoid reminders of the incident with the client?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve been changing my routine in any significant way. I still go about my day pretty much the same. I mean, I might feel a little uneasy if something brings it to mind, but I’m not rearranging my life around it or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, a little, I guess. I wasn’t sure if that was something to mention because it sounds strange, but that’s kind of what brought me in. I’ll be at home or in my office and suddenly it feels… not real, almost like I’m watching myself from across the room instead of being fully there. It passes, but it’s unsettling. Does that fit what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been staying away from places or activities that remind you of the client who threatened you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t been avoiding places or activities because of him, at least not that I’ve noticed. I still go where I need to go and do the usual things. I guess I might feel a little tense if something reminds me of it, but I’m not staying away from anything on purpose. Does that answer it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
